--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to disconnect the two black nodes (G and I), we need to identify all the paths between these two nodes and find the bottlenecks (nodes) that, when removed, will disconnect all paths.

Let's start by identifying the paths between nodes G and I:

1. **Direct Paths:**
   - G -> H -> I
   - G -> F -> J -> I
   - G -> F -> X -> J -> I
   - G -> F -> K -> J -> I

2. **Indirect Paths:**
   - G -> H -> C -> I
   - G -> F -> X -> K -> J -> I
   - G -> F -> X -> K -> P -> C -> I
   - G -> H -> B -> C -> I

Next, we need to identify the nodes that, when removed, will disconnect all paths between G and I. We observe the following bottlenecks:

- Node H: Removing H disconnects the path G -> H -> I and G -> H -> B -> C -> I.
- Node F: Removing F disconnects the paths G -> F -> J -> I, G -> F -> X -> J -> I, and G -> F -> K -> J -> I.
- Node J: Removing J disconnects the paths G -> F -> J -> I, G -> F -> X -> J -> I, and G -> F -> K -> J -> I.
- Node C: Removing C disconnects the paths G -> H -> C -> I and G -> F -> X -> K -> P -> C -> I.

To minimize the number of nodes removed, we need to find the smallest set of nodes whose removal will disconnect all paths. We observe that removing nodes F, H, and C will disconnect all paths between G and I.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed is:

Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [F, H, C]

   - The black nodes are E and H.

2. **Find immediate neighbors of E and H:**
   - E is connected to: F, G, B, M, and I.
   - H is connected to: B, G, and I.

   - Direct paths from E to H: E → B → H, E → G → H, E → I → H.

     - E → B → C → O → Y → N → H
     - E → F → G → H
     - E → F → X → G → H
     - E → G → X → F → G → H (this is a redundant path since E → G → H already covers it)
     - E → I → J → P → O → N → H

4. **Determine the minimum cut set:**
   - To disconnect E and H, we need to remove edges that cover all these paths.
   - Removing the edges (E, B), (E, G), and (E, I) will disconnect E from H directly.
   - However, this does not cover all indirect paths. We need to ensure all indirect paths are also disconnected.
   - Removing (E, B), (H, B), and (I, J) will break all direct and indirect paths.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges to remove is 3, with the cut set being [(E, B), (H, B), (I, J)].

   - Removing any one of G or X would block some of the paths but not all.

     - The first path through G is blocked.
     - The second path through G → I is blocked.

   - From E to B: Move to B
   - From B to H: Move to H

The shortest path is [M, E, B, H] with a length of 3.
